# Harnesses

Agent harnesses and orchestration runtimes: tool-use loops, model–tool boundary controllers, decision/context reuse, harness self-improvement, and the token/cost efficiency of agent scaffolding.

| Paper | Importance | arXiv | Authors | Published | Added | Summary |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Reflexion: Language Agents with Verbal Reinforcement Learning | ★★★★☆ | [2303.11366](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.11366) | Noah Shinn et al. | 2023-03-20 | 2026-10-26 | Agents learn from verbal self-reflection stored in episodic memory: after a failed attempt the agent writes a textual post-mortem and retries conditioned on it, with no weight updates — a founding self-improvement loop for language agents. Cited from the HarnessMM (2610.32813) reference crawl. |
| Harness Engineering: Anatomy, Architecture, and Evolution of Coding Agents — A Source-Code Study of Eleven Systems | ★★★★☆ | [2609.00006](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.00006) | Paul Barbaste et al. | 2026-07-15 | 2026-11-08 | An agent is a model plus a harness: a source-code anatomy of 11 production coding agents (Claude Code, Codex CLI, Gemini CLI, OpenHands, Aider and more) across 4M lines, decomposed into 7 shared parts — loop, model bridge, tools, memory/context, safety, orchestration, extensibility. Findings: none of the 11 imports LangChain/LangGraph/AutoGen; none uses vector search for code retrieval (ripgrep, globs, markdown instead); SKILL.md appears in 9 of 11, ahead of MCP at 8 of 11. Offers 18 design recommendations and a 90-line minimum-viable-harness scaffold. |
